### India Targets Journalists in Crackdown on Growing Protest Movement
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-19 21:20 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gen-z-india-protests-journalists-press-freedom-new-delhi-cockroach-janata-party-demonstrations-youth-journalists-detained-jailed/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

As protests continue across India over allegations of government corruption, dozens of members of the media have been subjected to arrest, detainment and assault, according to witnesses, press rights organizations, and the journalists themselves. Thousands of protesters have taken to the streets of New Delhi in recent weeks, demanding the resignation of election officials allegedly tied to the deletion of nearly 130 million names from voter registration rolls. The protests — which have largely been organized by the Cockroach Janata Party, so named after India's chief justice called the nation’s unemployed youth and activists "cockroaches" — are part of a wider wave of youth discontent aimed at Prime Minister Narendra Modi and his administration. Organizers have mobilized young people via social media, tapping into frustrations with government corruption, eroding trust in democratic institutions, and a lack of access to jobs and educational opportunities.

### Testing the Legal Limits of Press Access
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-15 14:51 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/press-access-trump-white-house-press-pool-political-cnn-reporting-president-justice-department-judge/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The Trump administration’s latest attacks on the news media have followed a familiar pattern: President Donald Trump lashes out at news outlets for perceived bias and follows up with restrictive actions that appear blatantly unconstitutional; a judge’s ruling follows, temporarily halting the actions; and the administration pivots and pushes ahead with other restrictions. This time it was the removal of three outlets — CNN, MS NOW, and Politico — from White House press access in September. After the groups sued, and U.S. District Judge Timothy Kelly issued a temporary restraining order restoring their access on Sept. 24, the administration barred CNN and Politico from providing pool coverage on trips outside Washington. Kelly issued a longer, preliminary injunction on Oct. 14, halting the administration from blocking the news organizations’ access to the White House while the case proceeds. Meanwhile, in another escalation involving TV pool coverage of the White House, the Justice Department announced Oct.

### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/independent-journalism-new-newsroom-launch-otter-news-daniel-drepper-business-plan-climate-crisis-environmental-journalism/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

### Labour accused of ‘climate vandalism’ by Green leader Polanski after Jackdaw gasfield approved – UK politics live
Source: The Guardian
Published: 2026-10-21 09:32 UTC
URL: https://www.theguardian.com/politics/live/2026/oct/21/jackdaw-gasfield-labour-andy-burnham-zack-polanski-uk-politics-latest-news
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The Jackdaw field will start supplying gas to homes and businesses from the end of the month, the government said Here is an explainer by Jill Ambrose about the Jackdaw decision. But environmental groups have condemned the decision. Ami McCarthy, head of politics for Greenpeace UK , said: Approving Jackdaw shows the toxic grip fossil fuel lobbyists still hold over our energy policy. Andy Burnham promised pragmatism [see 8.40am ] , but there is nothing pragmatic about rubber-stamping a project that won’t lower energy bills, won’t boost energy security and will create just 27 full-time jobs … True pragmatism means severing our reliance on expensive oil and gas that leaves British households broke, supercharges extreme weather, and leaves our energy security at the mercy of petrostates. Is the prime minister living in a parallel universe? One where we haven’t just endured our hottest summer on record, with droughts, wildfires and, in all likelihood, the most heat-related deaths on record? And where the UN hasn’t warned that we’re on the brink of breaching the all-important 1.

They were equipped with lanyards emblazoned with the word “victory”, a stock list of questions and a mobile app that mapped visited homes and the level of support in each. The volunteers were part of a mass student movement, Student List, that has rocked Serbia with two years of constant protests. On Sunday, the group will represent the most serious challenge Aleksandar Vučić has faced in his 12 increasingly authoritarian years in power, by putting forward their own candidates in parliamentary elections. 据新华社12月15日援引澳大利亚广播公司报道，澳大利亚联合反恐小组调查人员表示，悉尼邦迪滩枪击事件两名嫌疑人曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”恐怖组织。 报道援引联合反恐小组一名不愿透露姓名的高级官员的话说，警方在邦迪滩两名枪手车内发现一面“伊斯兰国”旗帜。澳大利亚安全情报组织6年前就已在调查邦迪滩枪击案两名枪手之一的纳维德·阿克拉姆，他与“伊斯兰国”在悉尼的恐怖分子有密切联系。 澳大利亚警方表示，枪击事件的两名嫌疑人是父子关系，使用合法获得的枪支发动了此次袭击事件。 澳大利亚总理阿尔巴尼斯15日表示：“今天下午4点， 我将把更严格的枪支法律列入国家内阁的议程，包括限制个人可持有或许可的枪支数量 。此外，还需对许可证进行定期审查。人们的情况会发生变化，人们的思想也会随着时间的推移而变得激进。许可证不应永久有效。” 此次枪击事件已造成包括1名作案嫌疑人在内的16人死亡、40人受伤。这是自1996年塔斯马尼亚州亚瑟港枪击事件造成35人死亡以来，澳大利亚发生的最严重的大规模枪击事件。 持有枪支许可证长达十年 据多家外媒报道，其中一名袭击者为24岁的澳大利亚公民纳维德·阿克拉姆，他在现场被捕，随后被送往悉尼的一家医院。另一名袭击者是他的父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆，他于1998年来到澳大利亚，被警方当场击毙。 澳大利亚警方随后证实，纳维德·阿克拉姆此前已被新南威尔士州警方和其他机构记录在案。其父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆持有枪支许可证，名下登记有6支枪，目前均已被追回。新南威尔士州警察局长马尔·兰永透露，萨吉德·阿克拉姆已持有枪支许可证长达十年。 阿尔巴尼斯在新闻发布会上透露，纳维德·阿克拉姆曾于2019年10月首次引起当局的注意。他补充说，对该男子进行检查是基于他与其他人有联系，但评估结果表明，没有任何迹象表明他存在持续的威胁或暴力倾向。 新南威尔士州州长克里斯·明斯表示，警方正在调查他们的系统是否存在故障，导致合法持有的武器可能被用于恐怖袭击。 枪手“性格古怪”，爱好打猎 据《卫报》澳大利亚版报道，纳维德·阿克拉姆在袭击前从事砌砖工作。他的同事表示，纳维德性格古怪，“没人和他亲近”。“（砌砖工人）在一起的时间很长，因为砌砖是一项非常枯燥乏味的工作，所以大家会聊很多话。但纳维德是一个性格很古怪的人……他工作非常努力，几乎没有休息日。” 尽管澳大利亚警方并未确认纳维德·阿克拉姆是否合法持有枪支，但据这位同事表示，纳维德经常外出打猎，曾提及在昆士兰州南部高原的克鲁克韦尔附近射杀兔子和其他猎物。这位同事还表示，就在几周前，纳维德告诉老板，他的手骨折了，所以要到2026年才能工作。

### 儿童手表“小天才圈”调查：点赞成每日功课，有商家可解除家长管控
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 14:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32177210
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

在校园里，孩子们时常举起戴在手腕上的手表，彼此一碰，便加为好友。 这种以“安全”为核心卖点的儿童电话手表，如今已超越通信工具的属性，悄然演变成一个拥有独特规则、社交层级甚至灰色产业链的未成年人数字社交圈——“小天才圈”。 在这个属于他们的“江湖”里，“大佬榜”备受关注，“扩列”（增加好友）成为新的社交需求，“点主”（在主页点赞）是每日必做的功课……这些让成年人感到陌生的词汇，正构筑着未成年人的数字社交世界。 然而，伴随着这种社交形态的兴起，家长们的担忧也日益加剧：孩子过度沉迷手表社交、不良信息传播、因品牌壁垒导致的“社交绑架”等问题逐渐浮现。《法治日报》记者近日对该现象展开调查，试图揭开这一未成年人数字社交圈的真实面貌。 点赞成社交“硬通货” 据北京“小天才圈”资深玩家陈曦（化名）介绍，圈内有5000余名活跃用户，多为11岁至17岁的学生，这个社交圈形成了一套精心设计的“混圈规则”。“入圈”需设定两个字的独特圈名，随后可在平台分享生活点滴，吸引具有相同爱好的好友进行互动；若想提升知名度或影响力，需每日经营主页并与好友动态互动。 记者注意到，在一些社交平台上，大量以“小天才圈交友攻略”为主题的内容应运而生，内容涵盖如何快速“扩列”、获取更多点赞等“实用”技巧，评论区有不少“留下ID互加好友”的留言。在这一社交体系中，点赞是这套规则的核心——平台设定每日主页获赞上限为3000个，若要达到“100万+”的“大佬”级别，需连续点赞近一年时间。围绕点赞数与知名度，圈内形成了清晰的“大佬排行榜”，点赞数也成为社交“硬通货”。 这种对点赞的追逐，已催生出相关服务产业。记者调查发现，某社交平台上，拥有数十万甚至上百万点赞数的账号，售价从几百元到上千元不等：100多万点赞的账号标价1300元，90多万点赞的账号标价1200元。 今年18岁的“灵师”（圈名），在2021年14岁时加入“小天才圈”，她的账号目前已拥有150个好友、超100万点赞量。她告诉记者，这样的账号市场价值约1000元。 “围绕点赞需求，还衍生出代刷赞、租账号、出售‘大佬好友位’等服务。”“灵师”进一步介绍，例如付费100元至180元，即可获得“bot”（记者注：“bot”是一种第三方开发的自动化工具，主要用于刷赞、修改记录和发送动态等功能，这类工具能帮助用户快速提升账号点赞数，从而在未成年人社交圈中获得更高地位）自动点赞功能——用户将手表寄给相关人员进行10天左右的处理便能完成安装。此后，发帖5分钟内即可自动获赞，还可以一键查询未点赞名单。

### 产地造假、年份速成、无视监管！央视曝光新会陈皮市场乱象
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 08:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32176166
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

近日，总台接到群众举报，反映陈皮市场存在年份虚标、产地及工艺造假等问题，千元一斤的“年份陈皮”亦可能名不副实。 茶枝柑新果皮冒充陈皮 广西浦北出现“速成工艺陈皮” 记者发现，直播间里售卖陈皮低价轮番轰炸，年份承诺掷地有声。 直播间也有主播爆料，八成市面上所谓广东新会陈皮实为广西陈皮。 广西钦州市浦北县是茶枝柑重要种植基地，其果皮是陈皮风味“越陈越香”关键，也是浦北陈皮唯一原料。2024年当地陈皮产业产值破60亿元，采收季大量鲜果加工晾晒成柑皮。 果农和收购商称，今年浦北茶枝柑鲜果收购价走低。按20斤鲜果出1斤柑皮比例，叠加人工成本，品相好的新柑皮成本约30元/斤，低端柑皮仅六七元，且当年新柑皮并非真正陈皮。 这家企业的负责人告诉记者，按行业共识与团体标准，柑皮需陈化三年以上才可称为陈皮，陈化时间越长价值越高，但他透露，自然陈化需长期积压资金，多数企业是难以承受的。 这家企业的负责人私下告诉记者，为缓解资金占用压力，一种名为“轻工艺”的速成加工方式，眼下正在部分陈皮生产企业里流行，他们仅需一个月的时间，就能将新采收的茶枝柑果皮处理为外观近似三年、五年自然陈化过的陈皮，这样做能大幅缩短陈化周期、降低企业的运营成本。 浦北自然陈化五年陈皮批发价每斤150元至200元，企业造假“工艺皮”成本仅70元左右，高额利润促使更多商家放弃自然陈化、铤而走险。 记者离开企业，在广西浦北县柑浦堂健康产业有限公司分拣车间，再次见到大批量造假“工艺皮”陈皮，同一批“工艺皮”因加工受热差异形成色差，被分拣标注为三年、五年等不同年份。 负责人透露，不同年份“工艺皮”本质相同，批发价均70元左右一斤，零售价差极大，标五年款卖500元一斤，标十年款卖1000元一斤，仅凭外观就能翻倍抬价，利润超成本十倍以上。 新会陈皮从浦北进货 “工艺皮”贴上陈皮标签以假乱真 浦北与新会陈皮原料同为茶枝柑，仅产地不同，新会较高生产成本使当地部分头部商家常年从浦北拿货，浦北成其重要原料供应地。 记者在柑浦堂分拣仓库看到，大量标注“新会陈皮”“新会特产”的纸箱正打包“工艺皮”，这些广西陈皮每日批量发往广东新会，造假供需链路已成熟稳定。 记者发现，浦北当地部分规模企业和小型收购商，已形成了这种“工艺皮”的造假供应体系，可自主加工或快速调配各类“年份”陈皮货品，个别企业年销新会原料数量惊人。 随后经浦北陈皮企业负责人引荐，记者见到多位专程从新会赴浦北采购的商家，他们坦言同时经营新会皮与浦北皮，外地“工艺皮”因价格低廉成电商供货重要选择。

### 《怦然心动》导演莱纳与妻子在家中遇害，警方尚未证实是其子所杀
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 07:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178391
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

当地时间12月14日，曾执导《怦然心动》《当哈利遇到莎莉》《危情十日》等影片的美国导演罗伯·莱纳（Rob Reiner）在他位于洛杉矶的家中遇害身亡。洛杉矶警察已对外证实，现年78岁的莱纳与他现年68岁的妻子米歇尔·辛格·莱纳（Michele Singer Reiner）的遗体一同被发现。 2019年，罗伯·莱纳（左一）与《当哈利遇到莎莉》的主演梅格·瑞恩、比利·克里斯托出席TMC经典电影节。 据悉，洛杉矶消防局的人员于当地时间12月14日下午3点38分接到医疗救助电话，赶赴位于布伦特伍德区的一处住宅中，随后发现两人已死亡。当天晚间，洛杉矶警察局在其社交媒体官方账号确认死者为导演罗伯·莱纳夫妇，将由抢劫凶杀部门主导调查。而好莱坞媒体TMZ援引执法部门消息称，“两人身上有与刀具造成的伤口相符的割裂伤。” 好莱坞媒体《每日野兽》称，有消息人士向他们透露，是两人的女儿罗米最先发现他们倒在血泊中；罗伯·莱纳当时已死亡，妻子米歇尔在救护车送院途中离世。 此外，《人物》杂志称通过多方消息，得知莱纳夫妇系被他们的儿子尼克（Nick）所杀害。不过，这一说法并未获得警方的证实。罗伯·莱纳与摄影师米歇尔·辛格相识于《当哈利遇到莎莉》拍摄时期，两人于1989 年结婚，育有三个子女，分别为杰克（Jake）、尼克（Nick）和罗米（Romy）。 2014年，罗伯·莱纳与妻子偕三名子女杰克、罗米、尼克（右三至右一）出席活动。 《人物》杂志还在报道中指出：尼克2016年接受其采访时，曾谈到自己长达数年的毒品成瘾经历。这段经历始于他十几岁出头，导致他一度流落街头。他表示，大约从15岁开始，便反复进出戒毒康复机构；随着成瘾问题不断加重，他逐渐与家人疏远，并在多个州经历了长时间的无家可归生活。 2025年9月15日，罗伯·莱纳在纽约出席活动。视觉中国 图 获取更多RSS： https://feedx.net https://feedx.site

### 演员何晴追悼会举行，其子追思母亲：北京这场雪像是为她而下
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 06:53 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178536
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

一场雪落，人间倩影化雪归天。 12月15日上午，众多剧迷粉丝冒着严寒赶到北京昌平殡仪馆久安厅，送别演员何晴最后一程。12月13日，这位被誉为“古典第一美人”的演员因病去世，终年61岁。 不到10点，殡仪馆告别厅外已经站满了前来悼念的观众和粉丝，有人胸前佩戴小白花，有人手捧鲜花，一位浙江的网友还托人送来了悼念花圈。 “何晴的作品在我们心中留下了太多美好回忆，她的演技和性格都让我们深深喜爱，这次听到她突然去世的消息，我们非常意外，来到这里也是一种悼念。”一位何晴的影迷说。 现场粉丝在悼念簿上写下对何晴的话，不到一会儿，悼念簿就已写满对她的怀念。一位观众用清秀的字迹写下：“亲爱的何晴，我们的童年、青春都在您的影视作品中得到陪伴。美丽可人的何晴，很痛心听到您的消息，我代表我的同学们来这为你送行。” 曾在电视剧《水浒传》中饰演卢俊义的演员王卫国也来到现场，神色哀切。他说自己和何晴既是邻居，也是多年好友，在他印象中何晴是一个温婉、暖心的人，没有架子，总是远远地和人打招，笑嘻嘻地说话。何晴生病时常给王卫国打电话，请他送自己去医院。即便病重时，何晴也保持乐观积极的态度，王卫国有些事情想不开，她还来开导自己。“何晴走了，我们都很难过。只要你记住她，她就永远还在。”王卫国说。 《水浒传》中卢俊义的扮演者王卫国 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青深情回忆何晴往事：“我们是小时候就认识的好朋友，从80年代到现在，有缘分做了这些年的朋友。她说话慢慢的，很甜很温柔。” 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青 在广大观众的真挚请求下，原本仅面向亲友的告别仪式临时对外开放，大家排着队，秩序井然地进入灵堂，与这位“荧屏中的温婉佳人、生活中的坚韧女性”做最后的道别。 久安厅内的屏幕上滚动播放着何晴的照片，厅内四周摆满了亲友的花圈，其中包括六小龄童、唐国强、翁虹、尤勇智等何晴圈内好友送来的花圈，还有《三国演义》剧组送来的花圈。人群中不时传来一阵抽泣。 何晴之子许何代表亲友致悼词。他回忆了何晴对抗病魔的乐观积极，以及作为母亲对孩子深深的爱和眷恋。 许何说，妈妈很喜欢下雪，经常在雪后穿一件毛茸茸的衣服外出赏雪。“今年12月12日北京下了第一场雪，我摸着妈妈的脸跟她说北京下雪了，下得好大，好像很久都没有下过这么大的雪。在我的心里，这场雪好像是刻意为她下的。12月13日早晨天空晴朗，白色的雪布满了大地，我妈妈在她最喜欢的早晨离开了大家，去了天国，远离了所有的疾病和痛苦。” 何晴，1964年1月13日出生于浙江衢州江山市。她是中国内地唯一一位演遍四大名著影视作品的女演员，被誉为“古典第一美女”。

### US: Order ‘No ICE’ Zones during Election Period
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-20 10:00 UTC

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image A voter carries a ballot during early voting in the New York City mayoral elections on October 25, 2025. © 2025 Olga Fedorova/AP Photo (Washington, DC) – The United States government should order federal Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) officers not to operate in the vicinity of polling places, early voting locations, or drop boxes during the midterm electoral period, Human Rights Watch said today. The presence of these federal agents could deter people from voting. “ICE officers’ abuses have already seriously affected US citizens who have been stopped, beaten, detained, deported, and even killed,” said Philippe Bolopion, executive director at Human Rights Watch. “Voters should not have to fear being caught in an ICE dragnet when they head to the polls.” In-person early voting is already taking place in the majority of states.

### Guatemala: Prominent Anti-Corruption Lawyer Fatally Shot
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-20 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/20/guatemala-prominent-anti-corruption-lawyer-fatally-shot
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image People stand near a framed photo of Guatemalan lawyer and anti-corruption figure Juan Francisco Solorzano Foppa, who died after being shot by unknown assailants on Saturday while visiting a sports center, during his wake at a funeral home in Guatemala City, Guatemala, October 19, 2026. © 2026 Cristina Chiquin/Reuters (Washington, DC) – Guatemalan authorities should carry out a thorough, prompt, and credible investigation into the killing of Juan Francisco Solórzano Foppa, a prominent anti-corruption lawyer, former prosecutor, and former head of Guatemala’s tax agency, Human Rights Watch said today. On October 17, 2026, armed assailants shot and killed Solórzano Foppa at the Campo Marte sports complex in Guatemala City. Another man, identified by authorities as Gustavo Adolfo Aguilar Gamarro, was also killed in the attack. Guatemalan authorities arrested two men in connection with the crime, but prosecutors said there was no evidence linking them to the attack and a judge ordered their release.

### Saudi Arabia: Man Executed for Childhood Offenses
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-19 18:15 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/19/saudi-arabia-man-executed-for-childhood-offenses
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Riyadh's general court, Saudi Arabia, October 20, 2008. © 2008 Fahad Shadeed/Reuters (Beirut) – Saudi authorities announced on October 17, 2026, that they had executed Hassan Zaki al-Faraj, who was convicted of crimes allegedly committed when he was a 14-year-old boy, Human Rights Watch said today. The Supreme Court has also apparently upheld the death sentence of another man, Yousef al-Manasif, who was convicted of crimes allegedly committed when he was 15 and 16, putting him at risk of imminent execution. At least three other men convicted of alleged crimes committed as children are on death row, despite allegations of torture, forced confessions, and prolonged solitary confinement. “The execution of Hassan al-Faraj is unconscionable compounded by alleged torture-tainted evidence and a well-documented record of unfair trials,” said Joey Shea, senior Saudi Arabia researcher at Human Rights Watch. “With the Supreme Court’s recent decision, Yousef al-Manasif is also at serious risk of execution.

Click to expand Image Lebanese lawmakers at the parliament building in downtown Beirut, January 9, 2025. © 2025 Hussein Malla/AP Photo (Beirut) – Lebanon’s parliament should amend the recently-adopted media law to fully remove all provisions in Lebanese law that restrict the right to free expression, Human Rights Watch said today. Parliament passed the law on August 11, 2026, but a new draft law introduced by 10 members of parliament on October 5 offers amendments to the law that would resolve its key problematic provisions. “Given the history of public officials and powerful individuals abusing Lebanon’s laws to clamp down on journalists, activists, and artists, parliament should act to prevent further abuse,” said Ramzi Kaiss, Lebanon researcher at Human Rights Watch. “No one should face abusive retaliation for peacefully expressing their views, and parliament has a chance to end these abuses right here and now.” The media law, which parliament adopted after 16 years of deliberation, includes significant improvements.

Between 3Q25 and 3Q26, hybrid electric vehicles continued to gain market share, reaching 16% of light-duty vehicle sales. Growth of hybrid electric vehicle sales has slowed this quarter as the average transaction price for a new vehicle passed $50,000-the first time this price has been reached so far this year, according to Kelley Blue Book.

### The Why and How of Foreign Aid -- by Dean Karlan
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-21 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35926#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Foreign aid has become a melting pot of motivations and policies, where differently-motivated actors often converge on recommended policies while the similarly-motivated diverge. Breaking down the “why” and the “how” can illuminate real tensions and reveal false ones. The “why” asks which problems to tackle and who benefits: the recipient, the donor through economic, political, or soft-power returns, or everyone through global public goods. The “how” asks which strategy most cost-effectively addresses a chosen problem. Evidence is central to the “how”: use it where it exists, produce it where it does not. In particular, approaches which improve state capacity to use and generate evidence can satisfy multiple objectives: strengthen recipient countries, benefit donors, and provide a global public good of knowledge. Ultimately, the “how” is not a linear process, but rather an iterative one of learning, adopting, adapting, repeating, and then sharing so others can learn as well.

### Correcting for Heterogeneity in the Use of Subjective Scales -- by Daniel J. Benjamin, Kristen B. Cooper, Ori Heffetz, Miles S. Kimball, Jiannan Zhou
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-21 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35925#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Analyses of survey data may be confounded if people use response scales differently. We formalize and generalize the theory behind using “calibration questions,” such as vignettes, to correct for scale-use heterogeneity. Finding, in our (N ≈ 10,000) and existing data, that the mapping between respondents’ scales is approximately linear, we develop a simple estimator for scale-use-corrected regressions. We validate it using subjective-scale ratings of height and weight and illustrate it with life satisfaction regressions, where correction leaves the main qualitative findings intact but can substantially change coefficient ratios, for example tripling the estimated cost of unemployment relative to income.

### Too Little too Late? Nationwide Carbon Trading in China -- by Shuang Zhang, Douglas Almond
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-21 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35924#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

China launched a nationwide, rate-based carbon market in 2021. Covering the world’s top CO2 emitter, compliance depends on the intensity of carbon emissions rather than an absolute cap. We draw on the theoretical literature of rate-based systems to provide the first empirical evidence on an early adopter. Using plant-level data from satellite imagery and machine learning methods, we find that high-efficiency power plants increased electricity generation and CO2 emissions by approximately 5% relative to low-efficiency plants. Because its carbon market regulates nearly all coal plants in China, we focus on the differential responses by plant efficiency, finding no evidence of a differential reduction in emission intensity (though these estimates are imprecise). Relative to coal plants in Indonesia and the Philippines, which had no carbon market, China’s high-efficiency plants also increased emissions, while the estimate for its low-efficiency plants is small and insignificant.

### Staying Connected: Experimental Effects of Coach-Delivered Text Messages in an Adult High School -- by Alberto Ortega, Pietro Pellerito, Patrick S. Turner
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-21 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35923#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

More than 23 million adults in the United States lack a high school diploma, and most who return to school drop out before graduating. We evaluate a randomized intervention of adult high school students in which coaches delivered text messages that encouraged persistence. We find that assignment to treatment increased graduation by 4.9 percentage points, and the effect of message receipt among compliers is 9.3 percentage points, a 41 percent increase relative to the control mean. The intervention operated on the intensive margin by increasing the number of courses completed, but did not affect student enrollment. The effects of the intervention are concentrated among students assigned to experienced coaches, who customized their messages more extensively. We contextualize the economic impact of the low-cost intervention by comparing labor market earnings of graduates to non-graduates, which implies an earnings benefit of $149 per treated student over four years.

### Will Medical Care Strangle or Save the Federal Budget? -- by David M. Cutler
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-21 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35922#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Forecasts of the federal budget depend greatly on trends in medical spending. Since 1970, medical spending has nearly tripled as a share of gross domestic product (GDP). However, spending growth has barely exceeded GDP growth since 2010. This difference in growth rates makes forecasting spending difficult. I present scenarios for future spending derived from considering the sources of spending increase, ranging from rapid increases in new, expensive treatments to a reduction in spending due to cost-reducing technology. Even modest increases in spending relative to GDP are likely to overwhelm the federal government’s ability to pay for medical care using current tax instruments.

### Understanding Migration Responses to Local Shocks -- by Kirill Borusyak, Rafael Dix-Carneiro, Brian K. Kovak
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-21 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35921#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

A commonly used migration regression relates population changes to local labor demand shocks, often yielding small or statistically insignificant coefficients despite sizable effects on labor market outcomes. We argue that such findings need not imply that mobility is unresponsive to local economic conditions. Rather, conventional migration regressions can be biased, as they do not reflect how workers choose among competing locations. In a parsimonious equilibrium model, local population growth depends on differences between local shocks and those in relevant destinations; when these shocks are correlated, estimated responses are attenuated. Moreover, with industry-level shocks, standard shift-share measures misrepresent locations’ effective exposure, leading to further attenuation. Using simulation experiments based on Brazilian and German administrative data, we show attenuation can exceed 50% and that conventional regression estimates can be highly imprecise.

### Investment screening reshapes rather than shrinks foreign investment
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-20 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/investment-screening-reshapes-rather-shrinks-foreign-investment
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Driven by national security concerns, a growing number of countries screen foreign investment in an expanding range of sectors, yet evidence on the economic effects of such screening is scarce. This column uses new sector-level data for 43 OECD and EU countries to show that screening reduces the number of cross-border acquisitions by 11–18% but leaves the total value of inward investment unchanged. There is evidence for re-structuring of deals: minority stakes fall while controlling acquisitions rise. The effects are not concentrated on investors from China or other politically non-aligned countries.

### Lower capital requirements are no shortcut to European competitiveness
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-20 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/lower-capital-requirements-are-no-shortcut-european-competitiveness
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The European banking sector is known for high regulatory complexity, market fragmentation, and uneven implementation of international standards. However, this column argues that across-the-board reductions in capital requirements are not the solution. European requirements are not uniformly higher than US requirements, and capital rules do not mainly explain the past gap in return on equity, which is now largely closed. Lower capital requirements are also unlikely to unlock additional lending for early-stage technologies and innovative firms. Instead, reforms should focus on simplifying the interaction between different requirements, streamlining reporting, and completing the Banking Union.

### What happens to innovation after Big Tech buys a startup
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-19 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/what-happens-innovation-after-big-tech-buys-startup
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Concerns abound that ‘killer acquisitions’ by tech giants could deter startup entry and stifle innovation. This column analyses patent data before and after 1,200 acquisitions by eight technology giants between 1995 and 2020. On average, the ‘Big Eight’ firms appear to invest in the acquired technology rather than kill it. Acquired inventions become more influential, more widely cited, and more embedded in follow-on innovation by both the acquirer and competitors. However, longer-term changes to the competitive landscape need further investigation.

### Taming original sin redux: The role of benchmark-driven flows
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-19 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/taming-original-sin-redux-role-benchmark-driven-flows
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Emerging market capital flows remain highly sensitive to global shocks. This column analyses foreign investors in emerging market local-currency sovereign bonds depending on whether they are benchmarked to J.P. Morgan’s main local-currency government bond index or not. Benchmarked investors have increased their share of foreign holdings more than twofold over 2010–2025. Global push factors are strong determinants of benchmarked flows, whereas domestic pull factors determine non-benchmarked flows. Index design choices, such as country caps, can therefore materially affect flows to constituent countries.

### Here, there, and everywhere: Remote work and the employment of disabled and older workers in the UK
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-18 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/here-there-and-everywhere-remote-work-and-employment-disabled-and-older-workers-uk
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Disabled and older adults have lower employment rates than other workers. Policymakers increasingly point to remote work as a way to keep these groups attached to the labour market. This column uses the pandemic-driven rise in working from home in the UK as a quasi-experiment to explore remote work’s impacts on employment and other outcomes such as commute times, hours worked, and wages. A one percentage point increase in working from home raises full-time employment of people with a physical disability by about 0.9%, close to estimates for the US, and raises full-time employment of workers aged 50 to 64 by about 0.4%.

### Forward guidance and the limits of public information
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-18 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/forward-guidance-and-limits-public-information
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

When nominal interest rates hit the effective lower bound, central banks turned to unconventional measures, with forward guidance emerging as a cornerstone of monetary policy. Drawing on economic literature and data on interest rate forecasts, this column assesses the impact of forward guidance on interest rate forecasts. Crucially, during forward guidance episodes public information proves less accurate than private information. Despite this inaccuracy, forecasters over-rely on public information.

### On Curiosity
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-21 08:11 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/MTb3Wngr7GtoPwAFp/on-curiosity
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

TL;DR: A short post where I talk about curiosity as it applies to my own life, my research, and AI safety as a whole Recently, I’ve started spending a lot more time learning about random (typically science-y) topics that interest me. This was something I used to spend quite a lot of time on, in later middle school and in high school. It was originally mostly for my own enjoyment. There’s a certain satisfaction that comes from learning something new about the world, and from seeing the connections between different things. Over time, however, some of that curiosity instead got redirected to a desire to win. I would study for competitions instead of for my own love of the subject, and perhaps for my own ego. What came along with this was that I would often not truly learn the subject, or at least not parse it in the correct way. I would try to download information and understanding into my brain as quickly as possible, watching lectures at 4.5x speed and doing thousands of anki cards a day.

URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/MwSuycdhuapwWudCL/we-are-at-war
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

As I was going home from the champaign airport after a very eventful EAG conference, I struck up a conversation about AI safety with my lyft driver. I thought to myself “It’d be good to see what the public thinks. Maybe inform him of the risks. It’s positive counterfactual.” But as I spoke to him he started dropping words like “UBI” and drawing analogies to power concentration and gradual disempowerment. I was surprised to see how much he understood already. The conversation went one way or another and he went on to talk about his experience in the military. He spoke about the efficiency of the military, how they function with clear hierarchies, straightforward agendas, and utter coordination. Amidst his thorough and proud explanation, I began to envy how the military is able to function so smoothly as a “well-oiled machine”. He kept talking about how life in the military is completely structured. How instructions are simple, people know what to do, and that they make sure to do it. I said to him “So none of your work is bottlenecked by other people.

### Circuit priors in NN-bayes: more results
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-21 07:44 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/oePzacZn6wNcJPvH3/circuit-priors-in-nn-bayes-more-results
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

We're posting lots of papers written with GPT-6 Astra on generalization properties of bayesian neural net learning. [1] The following picture emerges: For various reasonable priors on MLP weights, [2] the induced prior on functions is a circuit prior: for fixed depth, the ordering of functions by negative log prior is roughly their ordering by minimal circuit width. From a quantitative version of this statement, one gets the following sample complexity bound: if the function is implemented by a circuit of width , then conditioning on train data points is sufficient to get test accuracy. [3] These results hold as long as is realizable; in particular, for arbitrarily overparametrized NN-bayes. Probably the main caveat is that these bounds have exponential dependence in the depth of the circuit implementing . One can get only polynomial dependence in depth by restricting the prior to only neural nets satisfying a robustness assumption.

### Are we asking the wrong questions about dark matter?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-21 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/asking-wrong-question-dark-matter/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Whenever the topic of dark matter comes up, the most common question that people wonder about it is whether this mysterious, hypothetical substance really exists, and whether it truly dominates the matter content in our Universe as thoroughly as its adherents say it does. Some advocate for a position where dark matter doesn’t exist, and that there’s just a large amount of normal matter that’s unseen to hold the Universe together. Others advocate for a scenario where there’s only a “normal” amount of normal matter alone, and that instead of dark matter to provide the needed gravitational glue to hold structures together, a modification to the laws of gravity will do the trick instead. But those advocates are an extraordinarily slim minority of working scientists: a group of contrarians whose claims fail to explain the overwhelming majority of evidence.

Probably, lots of jobs will be gone soon. Probably, the smartest thing alive will no longer be human.” Those who kept reading only encountered more of the writer’s malaise: “Everywhere around me there is this incredible sense of freefall and of grasping. … I stop going to lecture; I can no longer justify the time, and no one notices in a 300 person class anyway. I spend most of my time in the research building instead. … If there are a few years left, I want to spend them fully, and this is what carries me through most days.” A few weeks later, he advised his followers: ‘you have 2 years to create a podcast in order to escape the permanent underclass.

### Dinners for AI Safety
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-21 04:05 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/rLtHDaLYHpNAvdSeL/dinners-for-ai-safety
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

I’d be pretty happy if we lived in a world where in every reasonably sized city in the US (and perhaps more widely) there was one (or more) local regular dinners where people talked about AI. Not meetup groups, though those seem maybe good, too. Dinners, in someone’s home, run by nice people, ideally potluck or otherwise home-cooked, where they invite friends, family, and people they run into on the street, to just talk about AI, perhaps with guides or what to talk about or financial support, perhaps not. I think (though haven’t deeply researched) that this kind of thing has been responsible for quite a lot of political change. Most famously feminist consciousness-raising groups where small groups of women met in living rooms, often weekly, and took turns talking about their own lives on a chosen topic, which is where we get “the personal is political.” Moishe House and other groups fund people to run Shabbat dinners, just so more Jewish community can happen (this doesn't aim to or succeeed in leading to political action).

Random footnote on this otherwise not very famous paper. (I say 2025 since the arXiv was last updated on 4 Jan 2026; technically this event could've happened in 2026.) They seem to have had adequate security/telemetry to figure out the source of this relatively quickly, though it's hard to be really sure based on the given details. Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-20 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/the-surprising-upside-of-envy/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

There was a distinct moment when I stopped being a happy-go-lucky little girl, one who could play for hours alone at the bathroom sink with a set of colorful plastic nesting cups, and became someone who felt left behind and unseen. I was six. I woke up early one Saturday morning in 1975 to silence, which meant my parents were still asleep in their makeshift bedroom — what was once the dining room. They had given me the sole bedroom in our small apartment in Evanston, Illinois. To get to them or any food in the kitchen, I had to cross the living room. But something was different. Our living room was full of baby things. There were pastel-colored items everywhere. Stuffed animal toys, baby blankets, clothes, bottles, diapers, pacifiers, a crib, bathtub, shampoos, soaps, and lotion. From the stack of cards on our small dining room table at the other end of the living room, I pieced together that my mother’s friends had thrown her a baby shower. I recalled that she had left to go somewhere the night before.

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

“If we had put some of the stuff that’s happening today into the film back when we were making it, the studio would have told us we were being ridiculous,” James McTeigue, the director of the 2006 film V for Vendetta , tells Big Think. “But V almost seems tame now, a bit like child’s play.” McTeigue sees the film as prophetic: “It was definitely pointing the way to where we ended up.” Few movies leave a legacy as large as V for Vendetta . Although it was met with mixed reviews and modest commercial success upon its release, McTeigue’s adaptation of Alan Moore’s graphic novel of the same name has never truly left the zeitgeist — and there are a couple of reasons. The first has to do with the film’s themes. Moore’s graphic novel — written in the 1980s and illustrated by David Lloyd — attacked the Conservative government of “Iron Lady” Margaret Thatcher. When the film version came out, many viewed it as a critique of Bush-era illiberalism in the wake of 9/11.

### MMX mission to reveal the origins of the Martian moons
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-20 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/mmx-mission-origins-martian-moons/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

It was way back in the 1600s — immediately following the invention of the telescope — that moons around planets other than Earth were discovered. Jupiter’s four major moons were spotted by Galileo himself back in 1609 and 1610, leading his contemporary, Johannes Kepler, to hypothesize that the planet in between us and Jupiter — Mars — must have two moons of its own. Yet for centuries, no Martian moons were discovered. The first moons around Saturn were found later in the 17th century, and then Uranus and many of its moons were discovered in the 18th century. Even Neptune and its giant moon, Triton , were discovered back in 1846. Only in 1877 was Kepler’s hunch confirmed : when Asaph Hall spotted Mars’s two tiny satellites for the first time. The larger and innermore of Mars’s two moons, Phobos, is only 22.2 kilometers across, while smaller, more remote Deimos is just 12.6 kilometers wide. Both are very close to the red planet: Phobos completing an orbit every 7.66 hours at a mean distance of 9377 km from Mars’s center, and Deimos completing one every 30.

### How to experience more of reality
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-19 13:53 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/perception-box/how-to-experience-more-of-reality/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

What is reality? People have debated this for centuries. Is reality objective — something definite that exists independent of us? Or is it subjective, existing only in our individual heads? Lisa Feldman Barrett, PhD, a University Distinguished Professor of Psychology at Northeastern University, argues it’s neither. Instead, she believes reality is relational. Relational reality explained So, what does relational reality mean? Barrett offers this example: Suppose you tell your therapist you’re mad, and they say you’re actually sad. An objective view might say there’s a correct answer — one of you is right, or perhaps both of you are wrong. Others would say the answer depends on each person’s individual experience. Barrett says it’s neither. “Reality may not be out there in the world, separate from you, and it’s definitely not only in your head,” she says. “It’s actually a combination of the two, which means that reality exists, but it exists in relation to you.

### Masao Adachi: Demystifying a Legend
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-20 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/masao-adachi-demystifying-a-legend
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Masao Adachi in 2025. Photograph by David Matthew Davis. Masao Adachi is a filmmaker whose reputation well and truly proceeds him. Born in 1939 in Kyushu, Japan, he moved to Tokyo to attend Nihon University, coming of age during the turbulent protests against the renewal of the mutual security treaty between Japan and the United States in 1959–60. He started making films with a student group and soon emerged as one of the leading figures in the burgeoning underground cinema scene. In the mid-1960s, Adachi became involved with the sexploitation industry, writing and directing several striking examples of the genre for Koji Wakamatsu’s production company. He was also collaborating with major members of the New Wave like Nagisa Oshima. The political dimension to his output became more explicit in the early 1970s, following a visit to Lebanon, where he filmed Palestinian guerrillas.

### Remake, Remodel, Remarket
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-19 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/remake-remodel-remarket
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Cinderella (Clyde Geronimi, Wilfred Jackson, Hamilton Luske, 1950). In the early 2000s, stores including Barneys and Hot Topic briefly stocked saucy T-shirts depicting classic Disney characters, including, as James B. Stewart describes it in the book DisneyWar , “an archival drawing of Tinker Bell eyeing her derriere in a mirror.” The shirts were the brainchild of Andrew Mooney, the executive brought in to revive Disney’s moribund Consumer Products division, and they so incensed Roy E. Disney—Walt’s nephew and the vice chairman of the company’s Board of Directors—that he sent Mooney a note accusing him of “positioning Tinker Bell as a prostitute.” Mooney’s job was saved by the peacemaking efforts of the company’s then-president and COO Bob Iger, and another one of his initiatives would go on to have more success: the Disney Princess. Encompassing toys, dolls, Funko POP!s, clothing, kids’ jewelry, accessories, decor, home goods, and other categories, the Disney Princess line of products is unified by a term that would quickly enough become ubiquitous.

### Movie Poster of the Week | F.W. Murnau’s “Faust”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-16 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/movie-poster-of-the-week-f-w-murnau-s-faust
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

This month, MUBI Editions, in partnership with the Cinématheque Française, is publishing an extraordinary document: a cinephilic Rosetta Stone known as “The Faust Bible.” When, 100 years ago, director F. W. Murnau completed work on his final German film and his most ambitious one to date, members of his crew compiled a scrapbook of illustrations and photographs that documented the making of that film, bound inside a prop Bible, and presented it to Murnau as a memento of the production prior to his impending move to Hollywood. Considered lost for many years, the Faust Bible was recently rediscovered and restored by the Cinématheque, and now MUBI and the Cinématheque have recreated this “stunning artefact of cinema history” and are publishing it under the title The Faust Bible: The Making of F.W. Murnau’s Masterpiece . Laurent Mannoni, Scientific Director of Heritage at la Cinémathèque française, who guided the restoration and publication of the book, writes: One of the most influential and pioneering filmmakers of the silent era, F.W. Murnau was known for the privacy of his film sets.

### What the Image Hides: Tai Katō and the Art of Partial Vision
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-13 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/what-the-image-hides-tai-kato-and-the-art-of-partial-vision
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

LOOKING THROUGH THE ROOM Blood of Revenge (Tai Katō, 1965). In Blood of Revenge (1965), Hatsue, a young woman bound by contract to a pleasure house, wants to see her dying father. Wearing violet, she bows low to the floor and begs Okatsu, who manages the house, for permission to leave. Okatsu reminds her that she has already been “bought and paid for” and is no longer free to leave on her own. The film’s director, Tai Katō, does not frame the plea as a simple exchange between two people. Hatsue is fully in view, but Okatsu sits deeper in the room, partly screened by translucent fabric. A man leans into the frame from the right. On the left, a woman is just visible in the shadows. She then crosses the foreground, briefly obscuring much of the action while the dialogue continues. For an instant, she is a spectator within the scene. A second later, her body becomes an obstacle to our own view. Katō does not organize the image around a single center. Four characters occupy different parts of the frame, and our attention keeps shifting from one to another.

Published: 2026-10-20 00:00 UTC

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The surviving members of Faust’s original line-up present four perspectives on the krautrock institution’s indomitable spirit, writes Abi Bliss in The Wire 513 Faust Fliegen Lernen Bureau B CD/DL/LP Gugaruz Bureau B CD/DL/LP MOI Bureau B CD/DL/LP Ungebunden Bureau B CD/DL/LP At the peak of their success in 1978, the four members of US rock group Kiss all released solo albums on the same day. This move – born as much from hubris as internal rivalry – backfired as sales fell short of the anticipated windfall. A world in which Faust ever acheived that degree of fame is hard to picture.

### Ludwig Göransson: Mythic Music and Entropic Production
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-14 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/columns/ludwigg-ransson-mythic-music-and-entropic-production
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Ludwig Göransson’s scores for Christopher Nolan’s The Odyssey (2026) and Tenet (2020) trade on diegetic and instrumental ambiguities that intentionally disorient audiences in time and space, writes Philip Brophy in his latest Secret History of Film Music column Bernard Herrmann’s score for Don Chaffey’s Jason And The Argonauts (1963) is an archly theatrical extravaganza that musically routes transatlantic pop culture to the Italian peplum genre explosion from the late 1950s. Though this wave of grand gladiatorial epics, produced in Italy and often featuring American beefcake performers as titular heroes, was waning by 1963, Herrmann’s triumphant entrance horns, cymbal clashing, galley kettle-drum pounding and multiple cascading harps sound more pompous and less foreboding than his more well known orchestrations. Yet this score captures the plastic veneer across peplum’s exaltation of classical architecture and ancient vistas. Peplum movies constitute one cinematic wave entranced by Roman Imperial glories.

### Invisible Jukebox mix: Liz Harris
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-09 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/audio/tracks/invisible-jukebox-mix-liz-harris
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Listen to the music we played to Liz Harris aka Grouper during her Invisible Jukebox interview in The Wire 513 Each month in the magazine we play an artist or group a series of tracks which they are asked to comment on – with no prior knowledge of what they are about to hear. In The Wire 513 it is the turn of Liz Harris aka Grouper. Here you can listen to a mix of the tracks our correspondent Leah Kardos played to Harris during the interview, which is published in full in The Wire 513 . To find out what she said about them, subscribers can read the interview in our online magazine library here . Or you can buy a copy of the magazine in our online shop . But first, a brief biography of our subject: Liz Harris is a musician and visual artist who has recorded as Grouper since 2005. Her music is often made with very little: voice, guitar or piano, one room, one simple recorder, yet it carries enough quiet magnitude to fill New York’s Town Hall and Brussels’ Bozar.

### From Hiding to Civic Presence: How Wastewater Plants Entered the City
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-21 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1186845/from-hiding-to-civic-presence-how-wastewater-plants-entered-the-city
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

When London's main drainage was completed north of the Thames in 1868, the Metropolitan Board of Works marked the occasion with a banquet held inside a sewage pumping station. Abbey Mills had been built over the previous three years by the engineer Joseph Bazalgette , working with the architect Charles Driver, laid out on a cruciform plan with eight beam engines, two on each arm. Its style has been read as Byzantine , Italian Medieval, Russian, Ruskinian Gothic and Moorish , and its twin ventilation chimneys, richly ornamented, rose more than sixty meters. The builder called it an elegant structure in a swamp and thought it might pass for a mosque or a temple.

As a token of appreciation, the presiding head monk promised the family a small private lot within the precinct. The family chose to build a house twenty years later.

### Both selfish and prosocial actors distort their impact on others, but selfish-serving spin seems to spread
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02584-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02584-4 From consumers buying fair-trade products to policy makers funding science, people often do not know exactly how their actions will affect others. We show that selfish and prosocial actors alike may distort their impact in self-serving ways, but selfish-serving distortions catch on more readily than prosocial-serving distortions and increase followers’ selfishness.

### Recognizing Indigenous peoples
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02619-w
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02619-w Settler states increasingly offer Indigenous peoples symbolic recognition, such as land acknowledgements, instead of political power or resources, yet the effects of these gestures are untested. Peyton and McNamee’s experiments in the USA and Australia find that land acknowledgements raise awareness of Indigenous peoples but reduce interest in learning more.

### Responsibilism and culture
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02616-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02616-z Previous research has widely associated East Asian cultures with higher levels of collectivism, which is often measured through self-report scales that emphasize warmth and prosociality towards all people. Talhelm et al. developed a responsibilism scale that captures duties and responsibilities in close relationships.

### Petition style changes with AI writing support but outcomes do not improve
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02579-1
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 14 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02579-1 In 2023, Change.org introduced an artificial intelligence (AI)-supported petition-drafting tool to their platform. With access to this tool, users’ petitions changed substantially, and their texts became more similar to one another. At the same time, overall petition outcomes did not improve, which shows the limits of AI integration into online advocacy communities.

Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 19 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02605-2 Xu et al. examine how people perceive faces as being associated with psychological traits, using a task that imposes no a priori labels.

### Motor abstraction training generalizes to the refinement of specific movement patterns

Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02597-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 19 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02597-z Practicing unfamiliar characters in rotated orientations improved upright writing as much as direct practice, showing that the motor system learns by abstracting a movement’s structure, not just its details.

### More current with less calcium: Computational models help solve longstanding ion channel evolution mystery
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-10-21 09:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-current-calcium-longstanding-ion-channel.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In the early 1980s, scientists observed an anomaly. Embedded within cell membranes, they identified ion channels with extraordinarily high conductance. These so-called "BK" (Big K, or Big Potassium) channels had roughly 10 times the unitary conductance (i.e., conductance per individual channel molecule) of other potassium channels, enabling much more efficient, rapid transport of potassium ions. And while later structural characterization explained how this happened, no one could understand why. That is, until now.

Researchers at Uppsala University have adapted Cell Painting, a widely used imaging method in drug discovery, to look at individual cells inside small 3D clusters of cancer cells. Two drugs that looked almost identical in ordinary flat cell cultures behaved differently in 3D, and the team linked the difference to how each drug switches on the cell's p53 stress response.

Scientists have uncovered a surprising reason why statins, the widely prescribed cholesterol-lowering drugs that help prevent heart attacks and strokes, can sometimes cause muscle pain, weakness, and even severe muscle damage. Using advanced imaging technology, researchers discovered that statins can attach to a crucial protein in muscle cells and force open a channel that normally controls calcium flow. This causes calcium to leak continuously, potentially damaging muscle tissue.

### Depth-dependent soil water-salt relationships in the Hetao Irrigation District, arid Northwest China: Implications for sustainable management
Source: PLOS ONE Biology
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0360976
Source meta: journal · primary · R4 · P4 · signal

by Yuanqing Cao, Huihui Wang, Xuan Chen, Li Zhu Soil salinization threatens agricultural productivity in arid gravity irrigation systems worldwide, yet the depth-stratified relationships between soil water and salt content remain poorly characterized. We monitored soil moisture and electrical conductivity (EC) at six depths (0–100 cm) across 15 sites in the Hetao Irrigation District, China, during the 2025 growing season. Our results indicate a depth-dependent divergence in water–salt associations. The EC-derived salinity index varied from 0.08 to 42.91 g/kg, exhibiting extreme spatial heterogeneity (coefficient of variation (CV) = 185.2%) across the salinity gradient. However, moisture-salinity relationships showed marked depth dependency. Surface soils (0–20 cm) exhibited non-statistically significant correlations ( r = 0.07), whereas deep layers (60–100 cm) showed a statistically significant but weak positive correlation ( r = 0.25, p R 2 = 0.06), indicating limited explanatory power. This pattern is consistent with a subtle tendency of downward salt redistribution.

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

### Real-time fMRI feedback guides self-generated cognition toward upregulation of hippocampal activity
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01191-7?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Van de Langenberg et al. show, across two studies, that humans can upregulate hippocampal activity using instructed or self-discovered mental strategies. Crucially, contingent neurofeedback is necessary for effective self-regulation via hippocampus-relevant strategies. Successful hippocampal upregulation co-modulates a process-related network, enhancing its sensitivity to event boundaries during a subsequently viewed movie.

Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01202-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Wang et al. show that host nitrogen-use efficiency (NUE) mediates arbuscular mycorrhizal fungal (AMF) responses to nitrogen enrichment. High- and low-NUE maize exhibit contrasting response patterns driven by soil acidification and root plasticity, respectively, identifying host nutrient-use strategy as a key regulator of plant-mycorrhizal interactions.

Oppenheimer, Gilardet et al. use ancient genomes spanning 200,000 years to reconstruct the evolutionary history of Pleistocene bovines from mid-latitude Asia. ### PAK-family kinases promote cell fusion irreversibility by preventing cell-wall repair
Source: Current Biology

URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01211-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Fungal cell fusion requires local cell-wall removal, but repair mechanisms can reverse this process. Coronas-Serna et al. show that PAK-family kinases prevent cell-wall repair at the fusion site during fission yeast mating. Loss of PAK activity permits cell-wall rebuilding and resealing of fully open fusion pores.

### Patchy global progress to nature recovery

Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41559-026-03217-9
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Ecology & Evolution, Published online: 14 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41559-026-03217-9 The Kunming-Montreal Biodiversity Framework (KMGBF), adopted in December 2022, sets out a mission for nations to “take urgent action to halt and reverse biodiversity loss to put nature on a path to recovery”. A report presenting a midway review of its 23 action targets and 4 goals, to be met by 2030 and 2050, respectively, will be published in October 2027. We spoke to Flavio Affinito, co-author of the report and technical expert on the KMGBF monitoring framework, and Emily Nicholson, co-chair of the Ad Hoc Scientific and Technical Advisory Group for the report.

Genome-wide mapping of RNA structure in murine norovirus reveals structural elements whose disruption impairs viral replication in cells and in vivo. ### [Review] Addressing threats to future global health—what is the right timeframe?
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00880-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Our decisions today can support or threaten future human health. Insofar as we protect the planet or invest in fruitful research on pandemic countermeasures, we promote future wellbeing and health, sometimes at the present generation's expense. As we warm the planet, accelerate antibiotic resistance, or consume finite natural resources, we might serve present-day wellbeing or health at the expense of future generations. To decide which such trade-offs are legitimate or advisable, we need to know how to balance the health interests of future generations against each other and against our own.

### [The Lancet Commissions] The Lancet Commission on 21st century global threats to health
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01714-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This report from the Lancet Commission on 21st century global threats to health lays out a foresight-driven, health-focused approach to identifying and prioritising major threats to global health over the remainder of the century. We argue that recent global shocks (including COVID-19, Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease, major geopolitical conflict, accelerating climate impacts, rapid shifts in development assistance, and the emergence of powerful artificial intelligence [AI] systems) show how quickly assumptions about stability can be overturned—and why governments and multilateral institutions must counter short-termism and localism with systematic, repeatable risk assessment and preparedness planning.

### [Articles] Cardiovascular safety of orforglipron versus insulin glargine in adults with type 2 diabetes at increased cardiovascular risk (ACHIEVE-4): a phase 3, event-driven, randomised, open-label, non-inferiority, active comparator trial
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-21 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01865-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In people with type 2 diabetes at increased cardiovascular risk, the cardiovascular safety of orforglipron was confirmed by demonstrating non-inferiority to insulin glargine for MACE-4. Gastrointestinal adverse events were the most frequent adverse event and most common reason for orforglipron treatment discontinuation with orforglipron, while clinically significant hypoglycaemia occurred less frequently with orforglipron than with insulin glargine. These findings support orforglipron as a potential once-daily, oral treatment option with established cardiovascular safety in people with type 2 diabetes and increased cardiovascular risk.

### On the Limits of a Space-Time Description and the Physical Meaning of Phase Space in a Nonlocal Continuum
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-21 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26876
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.26876v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: In a previous paper we derived an uncertainty relation for nonlocal fields by showing that the physical localization width in nonlocal quantum field theory is broadened by the response kernel generated from the entire-function regulator. In this follow up we will reinterpret that result as just the position-sector limit of a more symmetric statement as we did not take into account the inherent nonlocality of momentum. We find and we prove under normalized, centered response kernels for both position and momentum the variance laws and derive the corresponding nonlocal phase-space uncertainty relation. The result still preserves the conclusions of the original paper all while strengthening the interpretation as nonlocal quantum field theory implies not merely a minimal measurable length, but a finite phase-space cell. We also explore experimental routes to test the theory.

### Revisiting self-interacting dark matter with DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation observations
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-21 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26877
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.26877v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We revisit self-interacting dark matter (SIDM), originally proposed to resolve small-scale CDM problems and drive late-time cosmic acceleration. Using latest DESI BAO, Pantheon+/DESY5 type Ia supernova, and cosmic chronometer Hubble data, we constrain three representative SIDM scenarios including creation and annihilation. We focus on the annihilation of dark matter particles in model SIDM1; our findings indicate the effective annihilation cross section is $\log_{10}(\kappa C_1)=-4.32$ (with an upper limit of $\log_{10}(\kappa C_1)=-3.90$). For the SIDM2 model, the SIDM fluid behaves similarly to a dark energy component with a mildly dynamical equation of state ($w_{\rm{SIDM}}=-0.82 \pm 0.04$). We also quantitatively discuss the dark matter self-interactions in a more general SIDM model (SIDM3), which reveals a strong correlation between the annihilation cross section and equation of state of dark matter.

### Towards a General Theory of GPS Inter-Satellite Communications Between Moving Satellites on Arbitrary Space Trajectories, Accounting for General Relativity Effects
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-21 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26879
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.26879v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: In previous publications it has been shown that it is possible to calculate (in terms of zero and higher order elliptic integrals) the propagation time of a signal, emitted by a satellite, moving along a plane (or space-determined) Kepler orbit. 2. By means of a method of intersecting null cones, the propagation time was found for the case, when the signal is intercepted by a second satellite. The present paper has the purpose to generalize the above approach for satellites, exchanging signals and moving along arbitrary space trajectories. Previously, the important physical notions of space-time distance and geodesic distance (only positive and greater than the Euclidean distance) have been introduced. It will be shown that these notions can be introduced also for the case of general motion of the satellites, and more importantly, they have the same physical meaning. A generalization of the s. c. ranging equation for the case of a curved space-time has been proposed.

### Is the Kaniadakis deformation of holographic dark energy detectable? A non-flat analysis with DESI DR2, cosmic chronometers and Pantheon+SH0ES
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-21 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26883
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.26883v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Kaniadakis entropy deforms the horizon entropy through a single parameter $K$, and the holographic dark energy built on it (KHDE) reduces to the standard model as $K\to0$. We ask whether current data can detect this deformation when spatial curvature is free and no cosmic-microwave-background calibration is assumed. Combining Pantheon+SH0ES supernovae, with the absolute magnitude fitted against the Cepheid calibrators, 32 cosmic-chronometer measurements of $H(z)$ and DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations with a free sound horizon, we find that non-flat KHDE fits the data as well as flat $\Lambda$CDM ($\Delta\chi^2=-2.1$ for three additional parameters) but leaves $K$ unconstrained: the profile $\chi^2$ changes by only $0.47$ between $K=0$ and its best value, and the bound $K<0.67$ (95%) reflects the validity domain of the model rather than the data. Information criteria therefore prefer $\Lambda$CDM ($\Delta\mathrm{AIC}=+3.9$, $\Delta\mathrm{BIC}=+20.2$). The joint fit gives $\Omega_{k0}=0.012\pm0.

### Commutative Coarse-graining on the Sphere
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-21 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26896
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.26896v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Filtering by spatial convolution underlies coarse-graining and Large Eddy Simulation, the analysis of multiscale dynamics, and data processing across the sciences. Yet, as commonly practiced on the sphere, it violates a basic consistency requirement: commutation with differential operators, which is necessary for deriving coarse-grained dynamics and preserving the physics across lengthscales. We discuss the root of the problem, which is intrinsic to the curved geometry rather than being a result of metric terms, coordinate system, or numerical grid. A recently proposed generalized spherical convolution is, for a given scalar kernel, the unique linear operator commuting with the radial projection, divergence, and curl. Any consistent linear operator, be it spectral truncation, wavelet transform, diffusion, Green's-function inversion, or a machine-learned convolution, necessarily takes this form.

### JWST Tracks Shifting Water Clouds and Churning Gases on the Coldest Known Brown Dwarf
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-20 11:30 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/jwst-tracks-shifting-water-clouds-and-churning-gases-on-the-coldest-known-brown-dwarf
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

When picturing a star, typically people think of giant balls of burning gas in the sky that emit enough light they are hard to miss. Planets, on the other hand, come in one of two general categories - rocky worlds like Earth or gas giants similar to Jupiter. But there is a third category of astronomical object that doesn’t really fit either of those descriptions, and also doesn’t exist in our own solar system. Known as a brown dwarf, they are sometimes called a “failed star” as they form the same way stars do, but never reach the critical mass of material to create active fusion in the cores. They are also much too massive to be considered a regular planet. But a new paper from researchers led by Brittany Miles of the University of Arizona describes one similarity between the coldest known brown dwarf and some of the planets in our solar system - it has water-ice clouds.

### Shipwrecks can become thriving reefs, but may contain hidden toxic time bombs
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-20 23:40 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-shipwrecks-reefs-hidden-toxic.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Late last year, scientists in Germany published findings on a World War II munitions dump in the Baltic Sea. Some may dismiss the dump as hazardous waste, but to marine biologists it was something far more exciting: a thriving ecosystem.

### Octopus and cuttlefish suckers may have evolved by repurposing ancient developmental genes
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-20 13:30 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-octopus-cuttlefish-suckers-evolved-repurposing.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Cephalopods, like the octopus, have specialized arms and suckers, but how these structures evolved from the simple bodies of their mollusk ancestors remains a mystery. Researchers from the University of Tokyo compared gene activity during embryonic development of cuttlefish, octopus and snail body parts. They found that several genes associated with appendage patterning showed expression along developing cephalopod arms, but no comparable patterns in developing suckers. Developing suckers instead expressed Ets1, a gene associated with early embryonic development in mollusks. The findings suggest cephalopods evolved suckers by reusing existing genes in new ways.

### Oldest animals on Earth found to share a common wormlike ancestor
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-19 17:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-oldest-animals-earth-common-wormlike.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

New research from the University of St Andrews has shed light on the evolution of an important yet thus far poorly understood group of marine invertebrate animals—brachiopods. The work, published in Science Advances, presents the first comprehensive phylogenomic analysis of all five extant brachiopod orders and related lineages.

### Mollusk family trees play a key role in shaping microbiomes, researchers find
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-14 20:30 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-mollusk-family-trees-play-key.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A mollusk's microbial community has more to do with its family tree than its address. In other words, the composition of a mollusk's microbiome depends less on the environment in which it lives and more on what species it belongs to, say University of Michigan researchers.

### Five new species of freshwater mussels in southern US uncovered by researchers
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-01 22:30 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-species-freshwater-mussels-southern-uncovered.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A new research paper published in Zootaxa describes five new species of freshwater mussels in the southern U.S., providing the scientific foundation for protecting the most imperiled group of animals in North America.

### DNA reveals chemosynthesis genes in bacteria living on coral reef fish gills
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-09-27 12:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-09-dna-reveals-chemosynthesis-genes-bacteria.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Chemosynthetic bacteria have been documented in various marine environments and in association with invertebrate hosts such as annelid worms and mollusks, but their presence in fish appears to be a new discovery. The findings show that fish gills host a complex and specialized microbiome that is not yet well understood but could play an important role in fish metabolism, immunity and health.
